# SSD dual-benchmark protocol, 2026-10-27

The separately frozen `config/dual-benchmark-v1.json` applies only to newly
registered research. It preserves the original four windows/cost paths and adds
net CAGR strictly above 12% and a full-capital, matched-cost QQQ total-return
benchmark alongside SPY. The old stress requirement to also beat base-cost SPY
remains. Sharpe must exceed 1 and drawdown must not exceed 15% on every path.
Turnover, modeled orders and fees are measured; there is no invented turnover
ceiling. Net returns are after modeled costs and before personal taxes.

The accepted private SSD environment, original inputs and frozen ledger are
reused, not reinstalled or reset. Source admission verifies the original raw
quotes, adjusted panels, corporate actions, calendar and current primary issuer
identity. A current issuer page is not proof of historical constituents, and an
HTML country-selection page returned by a PDF URL is not a factsheet. Such
failed document attempts remain visible and are not bypassed. QQQ's first-trade
date is explicitly vendor-reported, not an independently established issuer
inception date. Raw documents, prices and daily account ledgers remain private.

The original bundle's README and manifest remain byte-for-byte unchanged; this
separate document describes the new protocol without rewriting that history.

The zero-trial diagnosis completed **2026-10-30T06:14:37.348659Z**.
Analysis intent
`31854604152d67d9406fd9c4b41d9447946693726f3d9581d6f1a79ed2076a65`
bound760source references and five implementation/validation references
before deriving actual risk results. The existing strict scalar-report
Adapter is reused;the frozen analysis and all original source files
remain unchanged. Private diagnostic and summary hashes are
`b992855b6da7f95ad6d4c3dac590956f126697c4058e555a356fb73dc5c30df5`
and`886fa646280538d8ec61e0dc17aa0a875982efbac8001076dac1f430ec3d04fb`.

All121saved decisions remain,including seven explicitly unmatured
records. The equal control uses its actual equal-expert vector,not
the rolling vector or rolling score embedded in the shared original
audit. Original pair weights and selection scores are read unchanged.
For114mature decisions,the same84training/42validation observations
produce684rule/segment risk records,each compared with the original
NETSPY/QQQ scoring books. All12already completed main accounts are
separately compared with their matched-cost,matched-calendar benchmarks.

Portfolio RF-excess variance is benchmark RF-excess variance plus
active-return variance and twice their signed covariance. The1392
sample identities agree to a maximum annualized residual1.91e-16;
the independent scalar covariance difference is at most2.17e-19.
This is an accounting identity,not1392independent trials or a
drawdown guarantee. RF-excess volatility and the original reported
raw-NET volatility are explicitly different measures.

| Comparison with saved RF-pair control | Training windows | Validation windows |
|---|---:|---:|
| Higher absolute RF-excess volatility | 59/114 | 61/114 |
| Lower absolute RF-excess volatility | 26/114 | 24/114 |
| Identical weights and absolute volatility | 29/114 | 29/114 |
| Median absolute-volatility increase, percentage points | 1.6668 | 1.0713 |
| Lower QQQ tracking risk but higher absolute volatility | 52/114 | 48/114 |
| Lower SPY tracking risk but higher absolute volatility | 26/114 | 26/114 |

Relative to equal weighting,absolute volatility is higher in83training
and85validation windows,with median increases4.4235and4.5137percentage
points. The active score is a return-to-tracking-risk heuristic,not
a minimization of tracking error or an absolute-risk constraint.

The actual saved accounts show why these measures cannot be substituted:

| Path | RF-pair absolute RF volatility | Active-score absolute RF volatility | RF-pair QQQ tracking risk | Active-score QQQ tracking risk |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10y base | 13.3109% | 16.4348% | 16.5367% | 15.5883% |
| 10y stress | 13.3667% | 16.4426% | 16.5127% | 15.5686% |
| 5y base | 13.6717% | 16.3043% | 17.4318% | 18.2713% |
| 5y stress | 13.6825% | 16.3574% | 17.4310% | 18.3008% |

On ten-year base,QQQ RF-excess beta rises from0.4054to0.5266and
the negative benchmark/active covariance offset weakens. Thus lower
active variance coexists with higher total variance. Five-year
tracking risk also increases;the heuristic does not uniformly lower
tracking error. All four active-score absolute volatilities exceed
both saved controls.

The26positive saved active scores have validation absolute RF-excess
volatility median15.2395%and maximum33.0671%. Their median SPY
RF-excess beta is1.1316;beta above1is sensitivity,not evidence of
borrowing or a replacement for the preserved gross-exposure check.
These descriptive overlapping windows do not isolate drawdown
causality,justify a post-outcome cutoff or validate another policy.

## Initial benchmark command

From the SSD workspace root:

```bash
. "/Volumes/Extreme SSD/us_stock_qr-research/migration/ssd-takeover-20261027/ssd-runtime-env.sh"
"$SSD_WORKBENCH/.venv/bin/python" -I -B -m us_quant.dual_benchmark_v1 \
  --freeze "$SSD_RESEARCH_ROOT/migration/research-activation-20261027/dual-benchmark-protocol-freeze.json" \
  --output "$SSD_WORKBENCH/reports/ssd-continuous-research-v1/benchmarks-20261027-v1"
```

The one-shot command refuses existing output, enforces the real SSD path and
research deadline, and holds a single-computation lock. It constructs eight
benchmark accounts with both funded accounting and independent `bt`, plus a
closed-form single-purchase check. Benchmark accounts are not strategy trials
or forward validation. Subsequent candidate accounts still require their own
actual preregistration and sanitized publication fence. Never rerun completed
accounts merely because a continuation message arrives.

## First measured benchmark hurdle

| Window | SPY base net CAGR | SPY stress net CAGR | QQQ base net CAGR | QQQ stress net CAGR |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 2016-10-06 through 2026-10-05 | 15.4620% | 15.4056% | 21.1624% | 21.1239% |
| 2021-10-06 through 2026-10-05 | 14.0409% | 13.5283% | 17.0740% | 16.4707% |

Consequently, 12% or even 20% net CAGR alone cannot pass the ten-year
dual-benchmark gate. The source-bound four-path comparison, Sharpe and drawdown
requirements still apply together. Each benchmark account has one modeled
purchase, costing USD5.9970 in base or USD20.9581 in stress, funded from its own
fresh USD10,000. These are historical reference results, not a strategy success
or a promise of future returns.

The aggregate-only public reference evidence is
[`evidence/dual_benchmark_20261027_v1.json`](evidence/dual_benchmark_20261027_v1.json).
It includes scalar metrics, turnover, fees, source identities and hashes, not
raw issuer documents, market prices or daily account series.

[`config/equity-first-regimes-v1.json`](config/equity-first-regimes-v1.json)

equal allocations to the four existing momentum/value/quality/low-volatility
style funds within equities, a 2% target cash reserve, the 200-observation trend
law and a 0.025 actual-holdings trade band. Eligible equity receives the
investable budget; the defense is separately trend-qualified GLD or actual BIL.
The second hypothesis caps equity exposure using prior observed VIX variance
relative to the current completed observation. It is not a new economic factor,

records both registrations at 2026-10-27 16:30 UTC (October 28 Beijing), before

The new immutable JSON journal is separate from the original frozen SQLite
ledger. The original 178 completed configurations, 94 recurring reviews,

The pair adds two registered hypotheses, not a new two-trial authorization cap.

21 rotating daily target commitments. Twenty-one comes from 252 sessions
divided by 12 months; it is not selected by a horizon search. Both need
21 complete source observations before issuing an aggregate intent.

of the old strategy. Source files, old code, the 24-event pre-study journal
prefix, four economic families, 200-observation threshold, 98% target invested
budget and 0.025 actual-holdings band remain fixed. A calendar-aware month-end
test must not mistake the end of a truncated file for the end of a month.

before accounts. Only one funded account trades each aggregate target: these
are neither separately capitalized shadow portfolios nor averaged CAGRs.

records both registrations at 2026-10-27 18:39:57 UTC, before target preparation.

difference is zero for month-end commitment and approximately 2.22e-16 for the
21-slot mean; eight prefix checks include a non-month-end final date. No

Monthly commitment does not prohibit intra-month drift rebalancing under the

## Fixed stateful price-channel study

The saved eight-path commitment diagnosis reconciles existing drawdown,
calendar returns, fees and ticket counts without creating new accounts or
strategy trials. All four ten-year commitment accounts first breach 15% on
2020-03-09, when the previous close's raw 200-observation equity intent is
still 98% stocks. Later losses also occur after that source is already off.
Thus merely accelerating exits from the unchanged slow source cannot be
claimed to address both mechanisms. These observations are descriptive,
not counterfactual returns or asset-level PnL attribution.

[`config/channel-regimes-v1.json`](config/channel-regimes-v1.json) freezes a
different state machine: enter strictly above the preceding 63 observed
closing levels, exit strictly below the preceding 21, and otherwise retain
state. Both barriers exclude the current close; equality retains state.
The first valid comparison initializes off unless an entry occurs.
State runs through genuine prehistory, without a reset at either formal
account start. The original 253-observation common target warmup remains.
Quarter/month horizons are fixed engineering choices, not fitted optima.

Both hypotheses use the original SOXX/four-style equity composition and
equity-first 98% budget. One retains the strict 200-observation GLD trend
defense; the other uses an independent gold channel, updated even while
stocks are eligible. Ineligible defense goes to actual BIL. The comparison
changes gold eligibility, not the channel horizons. The funded executor,
actual-holdings band, pending-order discipline, costs, delays, RF and all
four acceptance paths remain unchanged.

The [actual packet](evidence/channel_regimes_20261027_registration_v1.json)
records registrations at 2026-10-27 19:51:47 UTC before preparation of both
2,553-session target matrices. A separate sequential price oracle agrees
exactly, and eight prefix checks preserve every earlier target. This is
causal preparation, not a return result. At registration there are six SSD
registrations, four completed trials, two pending and 182 combined completed
configurations. No account is run before actual sanitized publication.

journal/receipt helpers and the original funded executor without modifying

## Fixed asymmetric option-exit latch

The completed saved-channel diagnosis finds the first ten-year base 15%
breaches on 2018-03-22, while both the prior-close channel and old trend
intents still hold stocks. The ten-year target histories have 115 and 86
asset-class intention changes. None of the four base paths has a
prior-close-channel-off/current-close-equity-held mismatch. Gold exposure also
appears at later first breaches. These are descriptive observations, not
opening-weight attribution, a fee-only effect or proof that a faster fill
would avoid the losses. The diagnosis adds no strategy trial or account.

[`config/option-exit-latch-v1.json`](config/option-exit-latch-v1.json) freezes
**one** new response/recovery clock after 184 exposed completed configurations.
It reads the original saved month-end commitment matrix, not a reconstruction
of its signal. At each completed close, the previously registered
`VIX >= VIX3M` condition latches all 98% invested capital into actual BIL,
including any gold allocation. Only a noninverted, actual XNYS month end clears
that state and restores the latest saved monthly intent. An inversion wins
on month end; a normal intervening day cannot restore risk.

The state begins off in genuine prehistory and is never reset for a formal
window. Source warmup remains undefined. The full original option-close
coverage, price/RF inputs, daily actual-holdings band, cash funding, pending
requests, costs and four-path gates remain unchanged. No pending request is
cancelled, and overnight gaps can still breach the drawdown limit. This is a
conservative portfolio response to an equity-option indicator, not proof it
forecasts gold, a new economic factor or a capital guarantee.

The [actual registration packet](evidence/option_exit_latch_20261027_registration_v1.json)
records `2026-10-27T20:31:30.392482+00:00`, before new target preparation.
All 2,533 defined target sessions match a separate sequential oracle exactly;
nine prefix checks include truncated non-month-end histories. The saved source
and original accounts are not recomputed. At registration there are seven SSD
registrations, six completed, one pending and still 184 combined completed.
No new candidate account had been evaluated at that point.

The packet-wide no-account declaration is explicit and required. A per-target
copy, when present, must agree. Missing or nonfalse packet-wide evidence and

asset moves and accumulated implementation costs have to be distinguished.

At each actual completed exchange month end, use the latest 252 daily
adjusted-close return scenarios and 252-session observed total returns.
Treat the equal-six-family core as one linear scenario exposure, alongside
SOXX, GLD, IEF, TLT and actual BIL. No synthetic fund-price history or old
strategy target is created or replayed.

The observed opportunity score subtracts BIL's total return over the same
horizon. The hurdle is the greater SPY/QQQ score. If it is nonpositive, or
exceeds the analytical feasible maximum over the declared sleeve set, the
model explicitly chooses BIL. Otherwise the first linear program minimizes
empirical 5% expected shortfall of **joint portfolio** daily losses. The
second maximizes the same observed opportunity subject to the primary risk
optimum plus a frozen numerical slack. Missing data, infeasibility
inconsistent with the analytical bound, optimizer errors or failed
certificates stop the experiment; none silently becomes cash.

Weights are nonnegative and sum to one before the unchanged 98% investment
scale. The factor core is at least as large as the SOXX sleeve, a
model-specific thematic-concentration safeguard inherited without a ratio
grid, not a permanent user constraint or a fixed half-portfolio allocation.
Total equity and defensive allocations are jointly variable. All six
registered economic-family funds receive positive targets somewhere in
each complete formal window; source-blocked stock definitions do not count.

14 nonpositive-opportunity and 33 opportunity-unavailable observations.

negative total net log gaps to both benchmarks in all four paths.

opportunity hurdle after outcomes. The five-year-base nonpositive group
has a small positive SPY log gap but a negative QQQ gap; the two

Fixed tickets account for most base-case costs, especially with smaller

fee-free NAV. The nonpositive-opportunity groups have no model orders
or fees in these saved accounts, yet they generally still trail QQQ.
Neither standby nor commission totals alone establish causal attribution.

## Explicit daily variance-disagreement proxy

The [new registration](evidence/daily_variance_disagreement_20261028_registration_v1.json)
fixes `dollar_option_daily_variance_disagreement_v1`. Actual registration
at 2026-10-28 08:38:21.714785 UTC precedes target proof at
08:38:22.737987 UTC and packet creation at 08:38:22.739559 UTC.
No candidate account or realized return metric existed at this fence.

The [Federal Reserve working-paper abstract](https://www.federalreserve.gov/pubs/feds/2007/200711/200711abs.html)
for the variance-risk-premium research explicitly ties its empirical
quarterly-return result to high-frequency intraday realized variance,
not daily observations. The
[published paper identity](https://doi.org/10.1093/rfs/hhp008)
does not validate a daily SPY proxy or this ETF allocation. The source
review therefore rejected a **replication claim**, not every possible
separately specified daily-proxy experiment.

This single exploratory rule has its own hypothesis. At each original
month end, calculate annualized mean squared SPY daily log returns over
21 actual sessions, including that close. Multiply the source equity/gold
odds, GVZ/VIX, by VIX-squared divided by that backward-looking variance.
Equivalently, the new equity share is GVZ-times-VIX divided by
GVZ-times-VIX plus realized variance, with all volatility units converted
consistently and the original 30%--70% bounds retained. The unit tilt
coefficient and one-month window are fixed before targets and outcomes.
Zero/invalid variance or missing quotes stops the study, not a cash fallback.

Read the source's existing guarded target matrix instead of regenerating
the old pipeline. Preserve its exact 131 monthly dates, total non-BIL
allocation, BIL amount, original dollar half-risk decisions and relative
SOXX/four-style proportions. Only the division of that unchanged non-BIL
budget between equities and GLD changes. The four known nonmonthly GVZ
gaps remain unfilled; no intraday or full-daily source readiness is claimed.

Execution uses the original source's **monthly** observed-weight pure
0.025 band, not the newer daily-feedback allocator. Initial funding,
USD10,000, USD1 tickets, 5/20bp costs, one/two-session delays, fractional
1x cash funding, RF, initial drawdown peak and four complete windows remain.
Independent verification will check actual monthly holdings gaps,
issued targets, pending dates and funded-account parity.

The target proof retains all four genuine registered factor exposures
in both formal windows, original BIL/non-BIL scale and monthly dates.
The independent scalar target difference is at most
1.5432100042289676e-14; five prefix checks pass. No old strategy account
or historical target generator was rerun.

The proxy may have no predictive value and is not a true variance risk
premium, sector-specific implied variance, forecast guarantee or paper
replication. All joint net-CAGR/Sharpe/drawdown/SPY/QQQ gates and wider
reference comparisons remain. Counts at preregistration were eleven
registered, ten completed, one pending, 40 paths and 188 combined
completed configurations.

## Symmetric stock/gold daily-disagreement information

The [twelfth registration](evidence/symmetric_variance_disagreement_20261028_registration_v1.json)

The eleventh rule used equity-side daily disagreement while treating gold
only through its implied quote. This extension adds GLD's backward
realized daily variance using the **same** 21-session window. Multiply
the original equity/gold odds by the equity implied/realized variance
ratio divided by the gold implied/realized ratio. The equivalent equity
fraction is VIX-times-RV_GLD divided by VIX-times-RV_GLD plus
GVZ-times-RV_SPY, in consistent decimal units and the original bounds.

When both disagreement ratios equal one, the original option allocation
is recovered. When RV_GLD equals GVZ squared, the eleventh one-sided
formula is recovered. These algebraic identities are not performance
promises. The experiment is a single information-set extension, not a
coefficient, lookback, boundary or cash-scale grid after outcomes.

Preserve the exact source BIL/non-BIL dollar scale, 131 monthly dates,
four-style/SOXX proportions, 30%--70% equity bounds, 98% target investment
and original monthly 0.025 observed-weight execution. Reuse the existing
parameterized monthly account adapter only for the new candidate's
targets; no old account is replayed. All costs, delays, RF, initial
capital peak and four-window joint qualification gates remain.

2.1815882433884326e-14; five prefix checks pass. Missing or nonpositive
variance/quotes stop the study. Daily ratios are not true variance-risk
premia, exact implied portfolio risk, intraday-paper replication or

## Nearest feasible execution point inside the unchanged band

The [thirteenth registration](evidence/band_projection_20261028_registration_v1.json)

target investment, all original source evidence and the existing trigger:
maximum actual holding/desired gap plus 1e-12 reaches 0.025. Otherwise hold.
Only the destination of a triggered noninitial trade changes.

Project actual closing weights onto nonnegative weights summing to
0.98 within each source desired weight plus/minus 0.025. Assets with
both current and desired weights exactly zero remain zero. The unique
minimum-squared-weight-movement solution clips current weights minus
a common budget multiplier to those bounds. A fixed bisection solution,
convex optimality conditions and an independent piecewise-breakpoint
oracle must agree before a target can be issued.

This is **not a minimum-fee optimization**. A synthetic USD10,000
example has an algebraically consistent two-ticket cost of USD2.750316,
while the unchanged funding engine selects a three-ticket result costing
USD3.750431 for the same absolute target. This does not invalidate old
results; it prevents falsely pricing a new policy using a different
fixed-point selection. The engine is not modified and its actual fees
are always charged. Boundary execution may increase ticket frequency
or sacrifice return even when weight movement is smaller.

The source dollar/BIL scale is preserved in **desired** targets, not
claimed identical in executed projected weights. Actual destinations
can differ within the unchanged band, including relative factor shares
and BIL. Initial source-center deployment retains genuine factor
participation. Price gaps and delayed fills can move realized holdings
outside the band; it is not continuous drawdown insurance.

cash funding, pending-request preservation, RF, initial capital peak
and four full-window gates remain. Synthetic projection, execution and
fee-contract checks precede registration; real holding-feedback

inherited 126-observation lookback with a 21-session skip contains 105
actual returns. SOXX gross wealth is its endpoint price ratio. Core

gross wealth; the remaining stock budget stays equally in four styles.

and old four-style ranking inside a fixed core. There is no fitted

## Separately frozen two-segment information study

[`config/split-session-variation-v1.json`](config/split-session-variation-v1.json)
asks whether an already admitted daily open adds useful price-path
information beyond the eleventh study's close-only squared returns.
It does not tune the failed composition shares, risk bounds, window,
coefficient or holding band.

Each of the same 21 completed sessions contributes two squared log
movements: previous adjusted close to adjusted open, and that open to
that session's adjusted close. Sum the two within each session, average
over 21 sessions and annualize by 252, **not 504**. The close-only
measure equals this measure plus twice the mean product of the two
movements, with the same annualization. A cancellation can hide
variation in the daily net return; persistent same-direction moves
can make the two-segment measure lower. This is not a guaranteed
conservative risk estimate.

Retain the eleventh unit-coefficient option-odds rule, original
30%--70% stock fraction bounds, half-SOXX/equal-four-style stock
composition, saved original dollar guard, BIL amount, 98% target
investment and every original monthly decision. This study does not
continue the fourteenth within-equity share rule. All original
USD10,000 accounts, funding, delayed opens, monthly 0.025 observed
holding band and four-path dual-benchmark gates remain.

The admitted panel contains 2,805 complete price sessions and all
131 source months have sufficient prior observations. Opens are
vendor-adjusted model prices under the existing raw-open times
adjusted-close/raw-close convention. Cross-day distribution
adjustments and weekend gaps follow that convention; these are not
authenticated auction prints or standalone overnight-trading returns.
Two coarse intervals are not high-frequency realized variance, true
variance premium or an imported literature forecast. A range-estimator
literature search returned a transport HTTP 504 and supplied no
verified paper or formula; its actual failure is retained privately.
No Yang-Zhang/Rogers-Satchell/high-low construction is claimed.

The [actual packet](evidence/split_session_variation_20261028_registration_v1.json)
records registration at 2026-10-28 11:45:21.052080 UTC, before target
proof at 11:45:22.071005 UTC and packet creation at 11:45:22.072293 UTC.
All 131 targets retain the source decision lattice. Independent
ratio-log reconstruction differs by at most 1.15e-14 in weight; the

## Universal-portfolio theorem and execution boundary

A distinct construction review checks Thomas M. Cover's **Universal
Portfolios**, *Mathematical Finance* 1(1), 1--29 (1991),
[DOI 10.1111/j.1467-9965.1991.tb00002.x](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1467-9965.1991.tb00002.x).
[Crossref metadata](https://api.crossref.org/works/10.1111/j.1467-9965.1991.tb00002.x)
and the first page of the
[author-hosted scan](https://isl.stanford.edu/~cover/papers/paper93.pdf)
agree on its identity. The PDF has 29 image-only pages; local OCR and
reading were limited to pages 1--3. OCR mathematical glyphs are not
treated as exact formula transcription, and no full-paper or proof
read is claimed. An author-index URL returned HTTP 404; this is retained,
not interpreted as absence of the successfully retrieved paper.

The inspected model uses long-only period weights, gross price
relatives and performance weighting over constant-rebalanced portfolios.
Its asymptotic growth-rate comparator is the hindsight best constant
rebalanced portfolio, not a finite ten/five-year net benchmark victory.
Page 3 also mentions a finite-sample lower bound: this review does
**not** deny that result or claim to have verified its formula or proof.
A lower bound relative to a comparator is not an unconditional promise
to beat that comparator at every finite horizon.

Three predetermined synthetic checks clarify the application boundary:

| Synthetic check | Actual result | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| Initial uniform weights; gross relatives 2 and 1 | Wealth factor 1.5 versus best constant portfolio 2 | Finite underperformance is compatible with the asymptotic comparator |
| Initial uniform weights; gross relatives 1 and 0.5 | Wealth factor 0.75; drawdown 25% | The growth comparator does not impose a 15% drawdown limit |
| One funded 98% target purchase at 5bp; USD1 fixed ticket | USD20,000 account ends at 19,989.205289; two USD10,000 accounts total 19,988.205779 | Fixed tickets break degree-one wealth scaling; their difference is USD0.99951024 |

For the same funding calculation with proportional costs only, the
scale difference is zero. This uses the **unchanged** pure funded
rebalance helper, not a new portfolio or a fee-free substitute for
qualification. USD20,000 is only a scale-test input; all formal
research accounts retain fresh USD10,000.

The inspected paper equations do not encode this repository's fixed
USD1 tickets, 5/20bp costs, 1/2-session delayed opens and monthly
actual-holding no-trade band. A gross mathematical wealth mixture is
not automatically the wealth of one executable funded account.
No Sharpe, drawdown, finite-window net CAGR/QQQ guarantee or new
economic-factor definition is imported.

This boundary does not prove that cost-aware universal methods are
impossible. Any future adaptation must first freeze its expert space
and prior, information/decision clocks, matured observations, numerical
approximation, funding and cost aggregation. It must then satisfy
the unchanged actual registration, publication and four-path gates.
No expert grid, weights, sixteenth model or historical return outcome
was selected by this review.

All 20 direct references, the 178-event SSD journal and original ledger
remain unchanged; the three checks use only synthetic inputs.
Private review SHA256:
`3d0f934e158f2b136ee98bdd99b89599e3ea4b03472e3fc8f81e7093650b28f4`.
Counts remain fifteen completed SSD hypotheses, 60 funded paths,
193 combined completions and zero qualifiers.

## Separately frozen continuous-simplex wealth learner

[`config/simplex-wealth-average-v1.json`](config/simplex-wealth-average-v1.json)
freezes one new construction after 193 exposed completed configurations.
It averages the complete three-sleeve simplex under a uniform
Dirichlet(1,1,1) prior, weighted by products of **already observed**
monthly gross relative scores. The sleeves are the four-style core,
SOXX and GLD. Core information uses the arithmetic mean of four fund
gross relatives from equal **starting monthly capital**, not a
daily-rebalanced index or a synthetic asset traded without tickets.

A normalized Dirichlet-moment recursion integrates this polynomial
exactly in algebra, evaluated in floating point. Its finite monomial
states are not sampled expert portfolios or a parameter grid. There
is no fitted prior, forgetting rate, temperature, lookback or historical
best weight. Independent Gauss-Legendre simplex integration uses the
degree of the observed polynomial to verify targets, never to choose
allocation parameters.

The actual first completed month end, 2015-08-31, is only an anchor.
The first learning interval ends 2015-09-30; no return is invented
for the initial partial month. At each original donor decision, only
completed intervals through that close are available, followed by
the unchanged one/two-session fill delay. Learning does not reset
when the independent five-year funded account starts.

The original saved dollar guard's BIL and total non-BIL budgets stay
fixed. The learned fractions distribute non-BIL capital among the
three sleeves, with the core divided equally among its four actual
funds. The old half-SOXX and 30%--70% stock/gold fraction bounds are
**explicitly replaced model choices**, not permanent user constraints
or modifications to old studies. Long-only exposure, full funding,
98% target investment and every user hard gate remain unchanged.

This is a gross-score learning signal coupled to **one** actual
net funded account. It is not a net wealth average of separately
capitalized experts, a transaction-cost universal-portfolio theorem,
predictive-likelihood DMA or a Sharpe/drawdown guarantee. Fees are
charged by the original monthly observed 0.025-band executor, not
silently omitted because the information score is gross.

The [actual packet](evidence/simplex_wealth_average_20261028_registration_v1.json)
records registration at 2026-10-28 12:48:33.777062 UTC, before target
proof at 12:48:37.998866 UTC and packet creation at 12:48:38.000534 UTC.
All 131 original decisions remain. The first and last targets use
3 and 133 completed learning intervals; maximum independent weight
difference is 3.34e-15. Five prefixes, both original formal anchors
and positive participation by all four registered factor families
pass. Twenty-four synthetic/lifecycle cases include an independent
140-period integration check and no-replay/publication/expiry guards.
These are not candidate account results.

At registration there are sixteen SSD hypotheses registered, fifteen
completed, one pending, 60 completed paths, 193 combined completions
and zero qualifiers. Actual sanitized publication must precede all

## Credit permission boundary and separate ANFCI source review

The proposed Baa--Aaa quality-spread question is not a reuse of the
old BAA10Y, which also contains the corporate/Treasury maturity
mismatch, or a replacement for restricted ICE high-yield OAS.
The primary [DAAA](https://fred.stlouisfed.org/series/DAAA) and
[DBAA](https://fred.stlouisfed.org/series/DBAA) pages identify daily,
not-seasonally-adjusted percent yields on bonds with maturities of
20 years and above. Their rating-basket difference is not automatically
a pure default-risk or duration-matched factor.

Both pages contain explicit proprietary copying/reuse restrictions.
No applicable new source-use permission was verified; historical
observation acquisition is therefore deferred. This is a permission
boundary, **not an HTTP refusal** or a claim that the earlier frozen
BAA10Y study had no permission. The old data, results and caveats
remain unchanged. No Moody's observations or restricted high-yield
series were acquired, reconstructed or obtained through another provider.

A separate question concerns the
[Chicago Fed Adjusted National Financial Conditions Index, ANFCI](https://fred.stlouisfed.org/series/ANFCI).
It is a weekly, Friday-ending, not-seasonally-adjusted **index**.
The source describes a financial-conditions component adjusted for
economic conditions; it is not the proposed credit-quality spread
or proof of independence from portfolio returns.
The [Chicago Fed's primary explanation](https://www.chicagofed.org/research/data/nfci/current-data)
describes 105 financial indicators, estimated weights and historical
revisions. ANFCI additionally responds to economic activity and inflation.
A current historical curve cannot stand in for past as-of observations.

The [public ALFRED form](https://alfred.stlouisfed.org/series/downloaddata?seid=ANFCI)
and [download help](https://alfred.stlouisfed.org/help/downloaddata)
were inspected without submitting a historical download. The actual
form advertises POST, plain index units `lin`, output type `2`
("Observations by Vintage Date, All Observations"), zipped CSV and
a 500-character entered-date limit. Its first listed vintage is
2011-05-25. This predates the research windows but does **not** prove
that every required vintage or fresh observation is available.

The saved prospective query plan reuses only the existing market
calendar: 131 original monthly decisions plus their 63-session
reference queries yield **225 distinct prior-session vintage dates**,
from 2015-08-28 through 2026-09-29. Six proposed batches contain at
most 40 dates each and satisfy the advertised text limit. These
are source-download batches, not strategy configurations or a restored
six-trial quota. The proposed weekly observation range ends
2026-10-02; the original ETF return cutoff remains 2026-10-05.

Before acquisition, the source contract and validation must be frozen
separately. Normal advertised-form requests, raw archive provenance,
README/column vintage identities, actual observation availability
and the proposed unchanged 14-calendar-day freshness limit still
need verification. Daily vintage cutoffs are not exact historical
intraday publication timestamps. Unknown gaps, stale observations
or failed requests cannot be replaced with current data, relaxed
ages or silent cash states.

**At that metadata-review stage, no ANFCI vintage archive had been
downloaded, no admission had passed, and no trading rule had been selected.**
The metadata and query plan are not strategy trials, performance
results or independent forward evidence. All 16 direct references, the 190-event SSD journal
and original ledger remain unchanged. Private source-review SHA256:
`7cc18b3faedf6b31cfadac9687c1912abccea0b4bbb96898a384cea45645a6e5`.
Counts remain sixteen completed SSD hypotheses, 64 funded paths,
194 combined completions and zero qualifiers.

archive/vintage coverage is not complete fresh coverage. No age
limit is raised, missing observation filled, date removed or stale
reading converted into a safe trading state. The cause of the
historical stale snapshots is not inferred from holidays or current data.

The acquisition history preserves two distinct technical issues:

- The preferred owned-browser preflight timed out and its control
  channel retried navigation. Retained traffic shows five normal
  form GETs returning HTTP 200 and **zero data POSTs**, not a provider
  refusal. The owned browser was closed. Before any archive request,

  credential changes.
- The first actual archive returned HTTP 200 after the v1 source
  contract was frozen at 2026-10-28 16:13:05 UTC. The dot-only parser
  rejected native **empty CSV fields**. Its failed contract, code,
  raw response and failure receipt remain unchanged. The retained

The separate
[`anfci-native-csv-v2.json`](config/anfci-native-csv-v2.json)
correction recognizes only empty and dot fields as absent. It does
not insert observations, change finite values or relax source,
calendar, vintage or freshness rules. Its code was frozen at
16:32:25 UTC after seven correction tests, before the five remaining
requests. The first request at 16:18:51 UTC predates that technical
correction and is explicitly reused, not redownloaded. Remaining
requests began at 16:33:34 UTC; the sixth unique POST began at

The original client/parser contract also retains its 19 offline checks.

collection or completed validation should be repeated.

## Separately frozen fresh-only residual-stress decision policy

[`config/anfci-residual-confirmation-v1.json`](config/anfci-residual-confirmation-v1.json)
defines one new hypothesis after 194 exposed completed configurations.
The [primary Chicago Fed Letter 386](https://www.chicagofed.org/publications/chicago-fed-letter/2017/386)
explains positive ANFCI as financial conditions tighter than typically
suggested by economic activity and inflation. This supplies the
fixed zero boundary, not a threshold fitted to strategy outcomes.
The article also documents a 2017 change from two-step to simultaneous
estimation, additional macroeconomic adjustments and a longer history.
Actual own-vintage values are kept; today's method is not rebuilt
backward into earlier snapshots. Exact historical method-switch
timing and full methodology continuity are not authenticated.

The new policy reads the **saved fifteenth target matrix**, not its
generator. Its original BIL 0/49% identifies the previously verified
published-dollar 63-session guard. At each original month end, with
a fresh current ANFCI reading, retain the half-risk allocation only
when that dollar guard is active **and ANFCI is strictly above zero**.
Otherwise restore its non-BIL composition proportionally to 98%
and set BIL to zero. A donor already at full risk is unchanged.
Stock/gold proportions and half-SOXX/equal-four-style stock composition
are preserved. This can increase risk; no net return or drawdown
improvement is presumed.

The original **225-query full-freshness admission remains false**,
including both stale queries and every raw archive. This distinct
trading-information requirement uses current ANFCI levels, not
ANFCI 63-session changes. It does not silently omit an unfavorable
return date or claim that all source readings became fresh.

Before any strategy outcome, the following operational rule is fixed:
a current reading older than 14 calendar days produces an explicit
source-abstention record and **no new target/request**. It is never
interpreted as zero or a safe macro regime, even if the dollar
condition would otherwise be false. Actual holdings and idle cash
continue; previously queued fills are not canceled. All returns,
fees and mark-to-market dates remain. Unknown hashes, malformed
records, missing query rows or budget errors stop the study instead.
Both initial anchors must have an eligible latest scheduled target.

This follows the earlier repository's explicitly registered source-pause
pattern, but does not replay the older NFCI strategy or source audit.
The new ANFCI residual-stress channel and fifteenth price budget make
it a separate whole-model comparison, not isolated causal factor alpha.
No prior, temperature, forgetting rate, stock bound, source-age
limit, dollar window, reduction scale or trade band is swept.

records registration at 2026-10-28 17:37:56.489261 UTC, target proof

## Eighteenth construction: ordered training path, failed preparation

[`config/ordered-path-growth-v1.json`](config/ordered-path-growth-v1.json)
freezes a price-only construction rather than another macro-confirmation
or guard-threshold revision. At genuine month ends with 253 completed
closes, it constructs constant-unit training paths for the fixed stock
basket, GLD and BIL. The stock basket starts with half SOXX and equal
shares of MTUM, VLUE, QUAL and USMV in its other half; it is not a
daily-rebalanced synthetic fund.

ETF weights sum to 98% of total capital, leaving 2% zero-interest
idle cash. The 14.7% minimum stock allocation comes from the earlier
guarded minimum, not a new user-supplied constraint. The primary LP
maximizes terminal observed training wealth subject to all **31,878
ordered peak-to-later-trough inequalities** limiting that training
wealth's decline to 15%. A secondary LP maximizes BIL within `1e-10`
of the primary optimum. Chronological order and `highs-ds` are fixed.
The metadata calendar allows 122 month-end opportunities beginning
2016-08-31; this is not a claim that 122 real targets were produced.

The gross constant-unit training bound does not forecast subsequent
losses or establish a bound for the actual monthly, cost-bearing
account. Original four-path net CAGR, Sharpe, drawdown and dual-return
benchmark requirements, costs, delays and the 0.025 observed-holding
execution band remain unchanged. The original source loader still
checks inherited dependencies, but macro and option values are not
allocation inputs. No new source observations were acquired.

## Wealth-feedback prior art and guarantee boundaries

The saved-risk finding does not mean that using actual account
wealth is itself a new idea in this repository. The registered
[`growth-portfolio-protection` policy](config/growth-portfolio-protection.json)
and its implementation already ratchet a floor to 85% of the
highest actual closing NAV, including the initial USD10,000.
They keep the original execution delays and costs, use a 5-point
actual-weight band for the protected rule, and route reduced
non-BIL exposure to actual BIL. The policy and code hashes still
match both original registered candidates and their completed reviews.

These are the **saved old outcomes under their original protocol**,
not reruns or reclassification under the later SSD criteria:

| Path | Monthly-control CAGR | Protected CAGR | Control Sharpe | Protected Sharpe | Control DD | Protected DD |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10y base | 14.8420% | 8.4804% | 0.976249 | 0.689044 | 19.1579% | 12.8220% |
| 10y stress | 14.4354% | 6.2396% | 0.946958 | 0.479090 | 19.2587% | 13.5089% |
| 5y base | 17.6998% | 10.6431% | 1.000447 | 0.697345 | 19.3078% | 12.7959% |
| 5y stress | 17.0038% | 8.7843% | 0.954776 | 0.524623 | 19.4082% | 12.9568% |

All protected drawdown gates passed historically, but all protected
Sharpe and SPY gates failed. Neither that fact nor the old policy's
CPPI motivation authorizes a new trial formed merely by renaming the
same cushion rule or varying its multiplier, band or satellite.

### Primary source and the shared scalar factor

The reviewed primary source is Chung-Han Hsieh and B. Ross Barmish,
*On Drawdown-Modulated Feedback Control in Stock Trading* (2017),
[arXiv:1710.01503v1](https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.01503v1),
[DOI 10.1016/j.ifacol.2017.08.167](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ifacol.2017.08.167).
The advertised abstract page and seven-page PDF were retained
privately with actual HTTP200 receipts and hashes. Raw paper
content is not republished here.

For current wealth $V$, running peak $H$, limit $L$ and current
drawdown $d=1-V/H$, the paper's Section 5 modulator is

$$
M=\frac{L-d}{1-d}=\frac{V-(1-L)H}{V}.
$$

The existing repository implementation scales its non-BIL monthly
target by

$$
s=\operatorname{clip}\!\left(\frac{M}{L},0,1\right),
\qquad w_{\text{non-BIL}}=0.98s.
$$

Inside $0\le d\le L$, its total non-BIL exposure therefore has the
paper's scalar form $\gamma M$ with $\gamma=0.98/L=98/15$ for
$L=0.15$. This gain is **not** 6.53-times asset leverage: target
exposure remains at most 0.98. Below the floor the legacy clipping
is an extension outside the lemma's maintained drawdown region.
Twelve fixed synthetic wealth states at two capital scales agree
with the existing pure budget function to
`1.1102230246251565e-16`.

This comparison concerns the **scalar budget factor only**. It
does not equate the old multi-asset allocator, its BIL routing or
execution with the full paper. The paper's gain optimization and
possible time-varying gains were neither implemented nor fitted.

### Assumptions that cannot be silently imported

Section 4 uses bounded returns with explicit support endpoints,
zero transaction costs, zero interest and perfect liquidity. The
recursion applies the chosen investment to the next return; it
does not model a stale queued target that fills after an intervening
price move. The Section 5 lemma explicitly says IID returns are
**not required** for its guarantee. Section 6's separate expected
log-growth gain optimization **does** assume an IID return law.
The Section 8 multi-asset if-and-only-if result additionally
assumes joint support at the vertices of its return-bound rectangle.

Historical sample minima or observed correlations do not establish
these future support assumptions. The project's real 5/20bp costs,
USD1 tickets, one/two-session delayed opens and non-overwritten
pending requests remain mandatory. BIL is an actual price-bearing
ETF, not the model's zero-interest idle cash.

Two exact rational **unit arithmetic witnesses** illustrate the
distinction. They are not market observations, forecasts, strategy
backtests or zero-cost repricings of any saved account:

| Synthetic case | Result |
|---|---|
| Start at 10,000 with 98% risky exposure; first return -10% | Wealth 9,020; unchanged units still carry 8,820 of risky value |
| Rebalance immediately between shocks according to the ideal modulation formula | Risky amount 3,397.3333; another -10% return leaves 8,680.2667, or 13.1973% drawdown |
| Instead, a second -10% gap occurs before the requested trade executes | Wealth is already 8,138 before execution, or 18.62% drawdown |
| At a floor of 85 against peak 100, hold 98% in a hypothetical defensive fund which then loses 0.1% | Wealth 84.9167, or 15.0833% drawdown |

The first example uses synthetic support bounds of -10% and +10%,
compatible with the stated gain bound; its failure is the changed
execution timing, not a contradiction of the lemma. The second
does not assert that BIL actually made, or will make, that move.
Both examples omit fees only to isolate the assumption mismatch,
and do not replace the user's funded four-path cost contract.
They also do not claim that the older protected strategy breached
15% in its saved historical runs; it did not.

The review's decision is therefore narrow: **bare cushion modulation
is already implemented, while the ideal guarantee has not been
established for this execution contract**. A distinct return-law
or gain policy would need its own causal definition and friction-aware
evaluation, rather than importing a paper's fitted gain or claiming
that all drawdown-feedback methods have already been tested.
No such candidate was selected here.

The review finished at **2026-10-28T21:56:14.905164Z**. Its private
result SHA256 is
`1a7df093b92d8079fc0f140b7adb68f3fa6f0630158c1e7f77f0eb8e2fcf0cb3`.
Six unit tests, fourteen bound local references and the retained
old-model API snapshot support the comparison. This is not a
new independent investment experiment. The original ledger,
216 SSD journal events, old verdicts, one-day forward models,
19/18/1 registration/completion/failure counts and zero qualifiers
remain unchanged.

## Separate VVIX information source

[`config/vvix-inputs-v1.json`](config/vvix-inputs-v1.json) freezes a
source-only question: whether VIX-option uncertainty can supply
information distinct from the already used VIX level and term
structure. It does not choose a threshold, risk state, ratio,
allocation or new economic-factor definition. The old near-term
source's explicit `VVIX_admitted: false` remains a correct historical
record and is not edited to manufacture earlier admission.

The exact new history URL was first found in the preserved official
Cboe history-page anchor, then corroborated by the current
[VVIX dashboard](https://www.cboe.com/us/indices/dashboard/vvix/).
Its page-specific resource description is a static HTML string in
`CTX[cSymbol]`; it was parsed as data, not executed as JavaScript.
One ordinary request to the advertised
`cdn-api.cboe.com/api/global/us_indices/daily_prices/VVIX_History.csv`
returned HTTP200. There was no host, user-agent, TLS or proxy fallback,
no retry and no existing-comparator download.

### Definition and timing boundaries

The dashboard advertises the
[selected broad-based, equity and ETF volatility methodology](https://cdn.cboe.com/resources/indices/governance/Volatility_Index_Methodology_Selected_Broad_Based_Index_Equity_and_ETF_Volatility_Indices.pdf).
That exact resource returned HTTP200 and was byte-identical to the
previously retained gold-volatility methodology, although the older
request used its `api/global/us_indices/governance` location.
Neither old bytes nor their acquisition time were changed.

Version **9.0**, revised **2026-03-30**, gives these VVIX-specific
rules: 30-calendar-day constant maturity; AM-settled standard monthly
VIX options; expiry terms shorter than eight days excluded;
nearest-term selection; Cboe Options Exchange **C1-only** midpoint
quotes; approximately 15-second dissemination during **09:31--16:15 ET**.
The current filter is 0.50 volatility points/30 seconds. Generic SPX
paragraphs in the shared methodology are not substituted for these
index-specific contract and data-source rows.

VVIX values are annualized expected-volatility percentage quotes,
not VIX futures profits, ETF returns, a variance-risk premium or a
crash probability. A 30-calendar-day horizon is not 30 NYSE sessions.
The final 16:15 quote cannot support a prior 16:00 stock-close
execution. Any subsequent strategy must define its information
clock before outcomes while retaining the original execution delays.

The printed information table lists first values as “Prior to 2014”
and launch as “February 1, 2014”; this is recorded as a **source-table
label**, not independent authentication of the first live publication.
The downloaded history starts in 2006. Pre-launch index values may
be back-calculated, and no full historical methodology chain is
claimed. The methodology also permits republication of a last valid
index value when calculation is unavailable; the daily CSV supplies
no intraday stale or revision flag. Calendar coverage alone does not
certify fresh constituent quotes or unrevised historical publication.

## First VVIX uncertainty-confirmation candidate

[`config/vvix-uncertainty-confirmation-v1.json`](config/vvix-uncertainty-confirmation-v1.json)
defines one fixed information hypothesis after 196 exposed completed
configurations. The question is whether rising VIX-option uncertainty
helps distinguish dollar strength that warrants the original defensive
allocation from dollar strength that does not. It is not a fitted
VVIX level, a new variance-risk-premium estimate or a wealth-floor
parameter rescue.

The candidate reads the complete saved fifteenth
`dollar_option_two_segment_variation_v1` desired matrix, never its
generator. At every original month end, both current VVIX and its
quote exactly **63 original NYSE sessions earlier** must be finite
and positive. The 63-session quarter matches the existing dollar
comparison horizon; there is no threshold or lookback grid.

The old half-risk budget is retained only when its original dollar
guard is active **and** the current VVIX level is strictly higher.
Equality or a lower value releases that half-risk budget to the same
98% non-BIL stock/gold composition. A source target already at full
risk is unchanged. Stock/gold proportions, half-SOXX/equal-four-style
relative equity composition and all 131 opportunities remain intact.
Higher exposure can worsen losses; no success is assumed.

The information clock is after the regular VVIX index close at
16:15 ET, before the original one/two-session delayed opens.
No 16:00 stock-close execution, source-age relaxation, filled quote
or artificial safe state is introduced. Archival first-publication
and revision limitations remain. ANFCI values and its old source
nonadmission are not used or repaired.

## DBC role and provenance review: no new asset admission

The next investigated question was whether direct commodity
exposure could diversify gold risk while keeping the core-equity
and BIL budgets, rather than weakening those budgets through
another VVIX confirmation condition. DBC is already present in
old macro/TAA universes. This review therefore claims neither a
new asset discovery nor new evidence from replaying those models.
The earlier producer-routing study used XLE/XLB equity, which is
not the same economic exposure as commodity futures.

The existing source was located at
`data/factor-round-20261007/base`, not the old CLI's unused
`data/dual-horizon/market` default. Its manifest, `DBC.csv` and
`raw/DBC.json` hashes match the preserved allocation registration.
There are **4,214 archived rows from 2010-01-04 through
2026-10-05**, with the original 2026-10-06 acquisition time.
Only identity, schema, date bounds and provenance were inspected;
no new full-source/13-asset adapter or strategy evaluation occurred.
No market quotes were requested.

### Actual documents, not search-summary labels

Search summaries mixed newer dates with older or mismatched
document URLs. The first accessible issuer PDF was actually a
**2020 Form 10-K**, not a 2026 prospectus. A canonical-looking fact
sheet link returned HTTP200 **HTML Country Splash**, not a PDF.
That response and failed format check remain recorded. No country
or investor-status assertion, audience acceptance, cookie change,
user-agent switch or transport workaround was used.

The [issuer's commodity page](https://www.invesco.com/us/en/solutions/invesco-etfs/commodity-investing.html)
separately and explicitly links the [DBC legal prospectus](https://pex.broadridge.com/summary.asp?doctype=pros&cid=invesco&fid=46138B103).
This is a different publicly offered legal document, not an alternate
copy of the gated marketing fact sheet. Its literal download link
returned the **2026-08-26 prospectus with a 2026-10-21 supplement**,
without an identity declaration. Raw documents, headers and
ephemeral download keys remain private.

The current prospectus identifies a Delaware statutory trust and
commodity pool, not a 1940 Act investment company. Its strategy
uses commodity futures linked to an excess-return index plus
collateral income, net of fund expenses. It is not a physical
commodity spot-return basket; index performance tables are not
substituted for the actual fund's adjusted-price history.

### Admission boundary

The prospectus says the fund does not borrow from lenders to pursue
its investment objective, but also discloses futures-margin leverage
risk. Its dated allocation table reports:

| Reported item | Date | Disclosure |
|---|---|---:|
| Futures-contract allocation, based on notional values | 2026-05-31 | 102.65% |
| NAV posted as futures collateral | 2026-05-31 | Approximately 9% |
| Index methodology change | 2025-11-10 | Prior index history could differ under the revised method |

Notional allocation and posted margin are distinct quantities.
Neither a no-borrowing statement nor paying cash for exchange-traded
shares independently proves a fixed unleveraged underlying exposure.
The single dated allocation is **not** adopted as a constant 1.0265
factor, used to rescale history, or treated as complete historical
notional evidence. Nor is a fixed 14-commodity basket assumed across
the original windows.

The conservative decision is **no admission to the proposed new
strict-1x study at this stage**: the required asset-role mapping has
not been established. This does not declare all DBC history invalid
or classify the product as permanently 2x, and it does not rewrite
the old macro/TAA records. No allocation rule, weights, 13-asset
panel, new candidate or account is created.

## Additional SPY range information: registered, not yet evaluated

[`config/overnight-range-product-v1.json`](config/overnight-range-product-v1.json)
defines one new information-field hypothesis: use the existing daily
SPY high and low observations to measure intraday movement that
opening and closing endpoints alone can miss. It is not a new asset,
another dollar-defense release rule or a window/coefficient search.
An annual relative-wealth proposal was not pursued because it
overlapped the already exposed relative-equity-odds family.

### Source and interpretation boundary

The [range-source evidence](evidence/spy_ohlc_range_readiness_20261029_v1.json)
binds the old `factor-round-20261007/base/SPY.csv` and raw response
to the immutable sector-growth market used by the fifteenth model.
All **2,805 original context sessions**, 11,220 raw OHLC fields and
5,610 adjustment/volume fields are checked independently.
Native high/low containment and the common adjustment factor hold.

Maximum JSON/CSV serialization difference is `5e-10` price units;
maximum difference from the already registered SPY price columns
is `1.1368683772161603e-13`, with volume exact. These use the
existing producer's price-identity tolerance, not relaxed investment
gates. The new sidecar preserves raw intraday O/H/L/C only.
Original adjusted opens, closes, raw closes, volumes and RF are
not overwritten, and no date or source gap is filled.

Rogers–Satchell is the bibliographic motivation for the range form,
DOI `10.1214/aoap/1177005835`. The ordinary primary publisher
request returned an unsuccessful Incapsula page; it was not
retried via another endpoint, header or mirror. Full primary-paper
inspection and theorem replication are not claimed. An AI search
summary's Yang–Zhang component/weight explanation was not
accepted as verified formula evidence.

### Explicit frozen proxy

For each of the same 21 completed sessions define:

```text
o = log(registered adjusted open) - log(previous registered adjusted close)
u = log(raw high) - log(raw open)
l = log(raw low) - log(raw open)
c = log(raw close) - log(raw open)
range = u * (u - c) + l * (l - c)
proxy = 252 * mean(o^2 + range)
```

The noncentered overnight component is retained from the fifteenth
proxy; only the intraday endpoint square is replaced. Common
same-day adjustment factors cancel in the raw intraday ratios.
There is no fitted mixture weight or newly selected lookback.
Valid OHLC ordering makes the range product nonnegative.
Range-only excursions can matter when open equals close, while
monotone bars can have zero range contribution. It is not
necessarily larger or more conservative than the prior proxy.
A zero/nonfinite total stops the version, not a default allocation.

This whole composite is **not** Yang–Zhang, a fully unbiased
day-variance estimator, high-frequency quadratic variation, a
true variance-risk premium or demonstrated predictive alpha.
Daily vendor extrema are not authenticated auction/tick records.
All information is used only after the completed signal session
for the original one/two-session-later opening execution.

The candidate reads the saved fifteenth desired matrix, never its
generator. It preserves each BIL and aggregate non-BIL budget,
all 131 monthly dates and the original half-SOXX/equal-four-style
relative stock composition. The existing pure stock/gold odds law,
native GVZ/VIX units and 30%--70% bounds remain unchanged.
Changing the measurement can still change risk and returns.

### Actual registration and complete target proof

The [actual packet](evidence/overnight_range_product_20261029_registration_v1.json)
records registration event **229** at
**2026-10-29T03:16:44.121982Z**, before actual new proxy values
and target construction. Proof event **230** follows at

## Paired core activity momentum: registered, accounts pending

[`config/core-activity-momentum-v1.json`](config/core-activity-momentum-v1.json)

execution contract. Both count as new configurations; the control
is not silently borrowed from a different old portfolio.

|---|---|
| `two_segment_core_price_momentum_control_v1` | Time-uniform mean of 105 adjusted daily log returns |

The score interval uses the already specified **126-session
lookback excluding 21 recent sessions**. Activity weights normalize
within each fund's own past window; scaling by their maximum is
only numerical stabilization. Constant positive activity produces
the price-control score. More activity can weight negative as well
as positive returns, so no bullish-flow interpretation is assumed.

All four funds rank by scores rounded to **12 decimal places** for
fixed numerical tie handling, followed by lexical ticker ties.

## Causal volatility HMM growth/BIL package: registered

[`config/hmm-growth-bil-v1.json`](config/hmm-growth-bil-v1.json)
defines one new allocation package, rather than retuning the
activity/range indicators or bypassing the blocked SMH source.
Earlier learned-return studies used ridge forecasts or adaptive
moments; this model explicitly estimates persistence of two
latent market-volatility states from admitted SPY prices.

### Statistical model and causal use

Each eligible month end uses **252 prior adjusted SPY log returns**
and two zero-mean Gaussian emission variances. The means are
fixed at zero: this is a volatility-state model, not a direct
expected-profit forecast. Initialization is deterministic and unique:
uniform initial state probabilities, a 0.95 diagonal transition
matrix, and prior-smoothed variance groups split by the median
observed squared return.

MAP-EM updates use a symmetric Dirichlet(2,2) transition prior and
inverse-gamma variance priors with shape 2 and scale three times
the same training window's pooled squared return. Equivalently,
transition counts receive one observation per cell and variance
updates receive six pooled pseudo-observations. State labels are
sorted by variance after each update, with transition axes
permuted consistently.

The penalized log posterior must not decrease beyond the fixed
arithmetic tolerance. Relative improvement tolerance is `1e-7`,
with at most 500 iterations and no alternate seed. Invalid,
unidentified, indistinguishable or unconverged fits stop the
version rather than supply a neutral or “safe” probability.
Per-month fit receipts remain available if a later fit fails.

Forward-backward smoothing is used only to estimate parameters
inside the **already known** training window. Only the filtered
state probability at its last observation enters the current target.
No later observation or newly smoothed past state rewrites an
older trade. A separate scaled-probability forward recursion
verifies each stored probability and log likelihood.

### Explicit growth/BIL allocation package

```text
p = filtered probability of the lower-variance component at the signal close
stock target = 0.147 + (0.98 - 0.147) * p
SOXX = stock target / 2
each of MTUM, VLUE, QUAL, USMV = stock target / 8
BIL = 0.98 - stock target
GLD and other ETF targets = 0
target idle cash = 0.02, with zero interest
```

The 14.7% lower stock bound is inherited from the earlier minimum
guarded-equity construction, not a user-supplied minimum or a
fitted parameter. It preserves meaningful four-style participation.
The original monthly observed 0.025 holding band, fractional
cash funding, USD1 tickets, 5/20 bp costs, one/two-session
opening delay, fresh USD10,000, RF and initial drawdown peak
remain unchanged.

Removing gold and replacing the old dollar/option budget changes
construction as well as statistical inference. Comparisons with
the fifteenth, price-core and older low-DD references are whole
models, not isolated HMM causal alpha or equal-risk ablations.
SPY is a proxy for market risk, not the exact conditional risk of
the concentrated stock basket. Neither fitted probabilities nor
convergence certify future profitability, global optimization or
the 15% drawdown requirement.

### Actual HMM funded outcome: stronger ten-year growth, joint rejection

preserves all metrics, execution statistics and unrounded gates:

|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|

| 10y stress | 18.369544% | 1.016944 | 14.165568% | 4.122249x | 486 | 2,389.48 |

| 5y stress | 17.845020% | 0.881524 | 14.222770% | 4.151278x | 234 | 798.43 |

All net-CAGR-above-12%, drawdown-at-most-15%, matched-cost-SPY
and retained stress/base-SPY gates pass. The ten-year QQQ gaps
remain negative by **1.383093 and 2.754320 CAGR percentage
points**. Five-year stress also fails excess Sharpe:
`0.8815240989001699` is strictly below 1. Only five-year base
passes all of its point gates, so the whole candidate is rejected.

The preselected price-core reference provides the complete
comparison rather than selecting only favorable HMM paths:

| Path | Price-core CAGR | HMM CAGR | Price-core Sharpe | HMM Sharpe | Price-core DD | HMM DD |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|

| 5y base | 21.0873% | 20.5968% | 1.243648 | 1.029539 | 12.5565% | 12.1539% |
| 5y stress | 20.1328% | 17.8450% | 1.180197 | 0.881524 | 12.5305% | 14.2228% |

HMM growth is stronger in both ten-year paths but weaker in both
five-year paths, with lower Sharpe throughout. Its fewer orders
and lower total fees versus the price-core model do not establish
uniform improvement. Against the equal-core fifteenth reference,
ten-year-stress fees are actually higher by USD61.88 despite
144 fewer model orders. Different wealth paths and notional
turnover matter as well as ticket counts.

The older low-DD reference is retained too: favorable ten-year-base
comparisons do not cancel HMM's weaker five-year-stress result.
These are whole-package comparisons, including removal of gold
and a different stock/BIL budget; they do not identify isolated
HMM alpha or equal-risk excess return.

Independent feedback checks cover 121/121/61/61 original decision
opportunities including initial deployment, with 80/81/40/39
issued requests. Maximum independent NAV difference is
`8.731149137020111e-11` USD. Cash stays positive and maximum
gross exposure is about 0.983186, below 1. No model refit or
source request occurs during account evaluation.

The fixed stress case changes both costs and execution delay;
the five-year degradation must not be attributed to fees alone
without saved execution evidence. The next investigation may
inspect those saved states and holdings, but not change priors,
transition assumptions, the window, floor or acceptance gates.
All 122 model fits remain frozen.

in target capital, not executed turnover. A continuous probability
blend does not imply small changes between monthly observations.
These descriptions introduce no new probability cutoffs, trading
bands or model-selection thresholds.

## Signed session momentum on a frozen HMM carrier: registered

The [actual packet](evidence/hmm_core_session_momentum_20261029_registration_v1.json)
registers three new configurations, not new economic factor
families. The question is whether signed nontrading and
regular-session return components carry different useful
relative style-momentum information from their full-day sum.
Information arrival and price discovery motivate a hypothesis;
daily vendor prices do not identify their causes.

Earlier SPY split-session/range research used squared intervals
for risk budgeting. Existing ordinary momentum, activity
weighting and information discreteness are not renamed as new
discoveries. The new comparison fixes:

| Candidate | Four-style ranking score |
|---|---|
| Full-day control | Mean adjusted close-to-close log return |
| Overnight | Mean prior adjusted close-to-current-adjusted-open log return |
| Intraday | Mean adjusted open-to-close log return |

All three use the same 105 returns ending at decision-relative
sessions D-125 through D-21, with the first prior close at D-126.
Their signed log components must add to the ordinary full-day
score. Each arm uses the original 35/30/20/15% shares of the
combined core budget, 12-decimal rank keys and lexical ticker
ties; negative or zero scores do not remove a factor fund.
The full-day control counts as a new configuration because its
HMM carrier differs from the earlier price-core study.

The exact saved HMM target matrix supplies all 122 source
month ends from 2016-08-31. Every noncore target is preserved
bit-for-bit, including SOXX, BIL, GLD and unused assets; the
aggregate four-style budget is unchanged. The HMM's priors,
fits, filtered probabilities, risk floor and total exposure are
not recomputed or adjusted. The 122 original model-fit receipts
remain hash-bound evidence, not inputs to another fit.
No extra source month is removed and the nine original undefined
prehistory months stay undefined. Both original formal account
anchors and all 2,512/1,254 returns remain required.

Prices retain the existing adjusted-open convention:
raw open multiplied by adjusted close divided by raw close.
Distributions, adjustment changes and weekend gaps follow that
same convention. These are not verified auction ticks, pure news
measures or realizable zero-cost partial-session trading returns.
Actual holdings receive **both** session returns, with the
unchanged monthly observed-weight band, USD1 tickets, base/stress
costs, one-/two-session delayed opens, fresh USD10,000 funding,
RF series and initial-capital drawdown peak.

All three registrations were written at
**2026-10-29T08:18:12.036214Z / 08:18:12.141068Z /
08:18:12.249005Z**, before any real component/rank/target
calculation. Proof events followed at
**08:18:15.650965Z / 08:18:15.753451Z / 08:18:15.854565Z**;
the packet was created at **08:18:15.856293Z**.
Independent scalar arithmetic reproduces every target exactly;
maximum unrounded score difference is
`1.2750217548429532e-16`, with log-split identity error
`4.336808689942018e-19`. Five chronological prefixes and both
formal four-family anchors are retained for each arm.

Twenty-nine synthetic kernel/lifecycle checks preceded real
scoring. Two first-run fixture errors remain recorded privately:
an intentionally changed synthetic price crossed the unchanged
60% data-quality guard, and a mock proof omitted the original
no-source-rebuild flag. Only the fixtures were corrected;
no production guard or economic rule was relaxed.
A separate attempted literature search for the already
implemented information-discreteness idea failed at the search
service with HTTP504. No primary text was admitted from it,
and it is not evidence for this study or an access workaround.

This carrier was selected after 201 exposed historical
completions. The contemporaneous full-day control separates
session information from ordinary rank tilting; the saved
equal-core HMM is an additional whole-model reference, not an
equal-risk executed-holdings counterfactual. No paper replication,
independent forward alpha or qualification is claimed.

At this preregistration snapshot, no new account has run.
The actual code and packet must be remotely published before
all **twelve** funded paths; every outcome and cost remains in
scope. Counts are **27 registered, 23 completed/reviewed, one
original technical failure, three pending, 92 funded paths,
201 combined completions and zero qualifiers**.

executed holdings, wealth or realized risk identical. These are

## VXN source readiness: archival Nasdaq-100 option volatility

The next step adds an information source, not a portfolio.
VXN reflects Nasdaq-100/NDX-option volatility rather than the
SPX-option population behind VIX. That distinction motivates
later research about proxy population; it does not establish
incremental forecasting power, exact SOXX risk or tradable
VXN investment returns.

The [actual source-readiness evidence](evidence/vxn_input_readiness_20261029_v1.json)
was completed at **2026-10-29T10:16:21.333057Z**. The official
[VXN page](https://www.cboe.com/us/indices/dashboard/vxn/)
was acquired once with HTTP200 at **09:55:43.744048Z**. Its
static description advertised the actual
[history CSV](https://cdn-api.cboe.com/api/global/us_indices/daily_prices/VXN_History.csv)
and the generic selected-volatility-index methodology.
The history request ran once, after the definition was recorded,

## Relative VXN uncertainty within the frozen stock budget: registered

The [new actual packet](evidence/vxn_relative_uncertainty_20261029_registration_v1.json)
defines one directional information hypothesis. Nasdaq-100 and
broad-market volatility levels are not directly interchangeable.
The rule compares each option quote with the already observed
movement of its corresponding liquid market proxy before
changing stock composition.

At each of the 122 saved HMM month ends, SPY and QQQ each use
the same original 21-session two-segment measurement:
252 times the mean of squared adjusted overnight and
open-to-close log returns. It uses 22 adjusted closes and
21 matched opens, not a centered covariance estimate,
high-frequency realized variance or unequal-duration clock weighting.
The existing pure numerical measurement is reused with explicit
SPY/QQQ audit identities, not with a false SPY label on QQQ data.

The two positive uncertainty measures are
`A_SPX = (VIX / 100)^2 / observed_SPY_variation` and
`A_NDX = (VXN / 100)^2 / observed_QQQ_variation`.
The SOXX fraction of the **unchanged total stock budget** is
`A_SPX / (A_SPX + A_NDX)`; the rest is shared equally by
MTUM, VLUE, QUAL and USMV. Both ratios are scaled by their
common maximum only for numerical stability. Equal normalized
uncertainty recovers the parent half/half split.
There is **no new clipping range, temperature, binary threshold,
satellite cap or within-core rank weighting**.

This treats relatively more priced forward uncertainty as a
reason for a smaller sleeve fraction, not as a promised higher
return. The direction is frozen before outcomes, not selected
from a sign or parameter grid. Earlier relative-equity odds
used past SOXX/core wealth on a different carrier, while the
earlier SPX split-variation rule changed the stock/gold budget.
Neither old rule or outcome is reclassified as this new study.

The risk budget is read from the exact HMM target matrix:
total SOXX plus four-style capital, BIL, GLD and every other
nonstock cell are preserved. No HMM fitting, filtering,
prior, window or stock-floor change occurs. All 122 original
months from 2016-08-31 remain; the nine undefined prehistory
months remain undefined, with no additional drop. Both full
formal account anchors and all 2,512/1,254 returns are retained,
with positive participation by all four existing style families.

The interpretation is deliberately limited. VXN prices NDX
options, **not SOXX options**; VIX is not an exact four-style
portfolio forecast. SPY and QQQ total-return ETF observations
are proxies for SPX and NDX, not identical price indices.
Thirty-calendar-day implied risk divided by backward
21-trading-session coarse variation is not a true variance-risk
premium or a portfolio covariance model. Equal target stock
capital also does not imply equal realized portfolio risk.

Only the admitted original 2,805-session VXN/VIX panel is used.
Both option inputs are the signal date's native closes and the
decision is after 16:15 ET, with unchanged one-/two-session-later
execution. This does not infer a live ratio from the differently
dated source endpoints, trade VXN index returns, repair
historical intraday-PIT limitations or change the forward models.
Fresh USD10,000 funding, 2% target cash, the original RF and
initial-capital peak, USD1 tickets, base/stress costs, long-only
cash-funded 1x exposure and the 0.025 observed-weight band remain.

Thirty-eight synthetic strategy/lifecycle checks preceded real
ratios. Actual registration at **2026-10-29T10:50:07.287294Z**
preceded all real variation comparisons, ratios, shares and
targets. The target proof was written at
**10:50:08.528775Z**, and the packet at **10:50:08.530791Z**.
Independent scalar price-ratio arithmetic and cross-product odds
give maximum target/share differences of
`2.4202861936828413e-14` / `2.4868995751603507e-14`,
variation difference `3.6637359812630166e-15` and relative

prefixes and both complete four-family account anchors are retained.

At this snapshot no new account has run. The actual code,

The prespecified comparison is the already saved equal-core HMM,
